D. EXPECTED PERFORMANCE AGAINST INVESTMENT CRITERIA  
This section refers to the performance of the project/programme against the investment criteria as set out in the 
GCF’s Initial Investment Framework .  
D.1. Impact potential (max. 500 words, approximately 1 page)  
DEFIS+ proposes a comprehensive approach to sustainably improving resilience to climate shocks and enhancing 
nutrition and food security for vulnerable households in the target areas. DEFIS+ will enhance the adaptive capacity 
of agricultural systems and vu lnerable rural communities to the adverse impacts of climate change so that farmers 
are better able to confront the increasing frequency and intensity of drought and changes in rainfall patterns that 
often result in floods. This requires modernizing the pr esent agricultural production systems, which are reliant on 
rainfall and whose infrastructures and practises are no longer suitable in cognisance of  climate change. DEFIS+ 
proposes to reduce smallholder farmers’ exposure to climate and economic shocks by climate -proofing irrigation 
infrastructures and rural roads to increase agricultural water availability, reduce the risk of market access disruptions  
caused by extreme climate events and promote and disseminate climate smart  agricultural practices. DEFIS+ will 
support 447,200  people,  of whom  50% are women.  
At the field level, the diffusion of water -saving technology such as the implementation of drip -irrigation systems and 
the SRI system allows for efficiency in the availability of water, reduces vulnerability to water shortages and increases 
productivity per hectare. This will increase the recoverable crop area that contributes to high levels of production. 
The support of DEFIS+ through the dissemination of drought and stress tolerant varieties and the adoption  of 
integrated pest management methods and other improved agronomic practices will contribute to further reducing 
production risk and sustainably increasing productivity. Through DEFIS+, the resilience of production systems will 
be strengthened by investing in ecosystem restoration,  including the promotion of an integrated landscape 
management approach (under the  watershed). This reduces the risk of erosion and will improve the sustained 
productivity of farms through the retention of fertile topsoils . Some activities such as the dissemination  of drip -
irrigation systems overwhelmingly benefit women who are the main producers of vegetable crops and will contribute 
to the nutritional improvement of households. Investments in  infrastructure reinforcement are aimed at the rapid 
evacuation of flood waters during extreme weather events and the reduction of risks associated with infrastructure 
degradation, thus ensuring sustainability and reducing maintenance  costs in the future. Finally, DEFIS+ will promote 
low-cost on - and off -farm renewable energy and energy -efficient technologies, such as improved cooking stoves and 
handmade briquettes, by using local materials, thus reducing deforestation and contributing  to carbon sequestration. 
Other technologies will also include solar pumps for irrigation, biogas production along with livestock systems, solar 
milling and dryers for product processing. These technologies will contribute to improving  efficiency and production, 
while also reducing women’s time burden and workload ( thereby  impacting their health, education, and nutrition 
status and indirectly those of their young children).  
By combining training on climate -smart agriculture with nutrition education and gender equality, DEFIS+ will 
contribute to improving the knowledge and practices of farming households to not only improve their agricultural 
performance and incomes, but also to link these positive outcomes with improved resilience to climate change, 
nutrition , gender equality and empowerment. By supporting the installation of automatic agrometeorological stations, 
the project will enhance the generation of climate data, which will feed into on -going initiatives that provide decision 
makers with updated climate information. Through awareness -raising sessions and formal training on the impacts of 
climate change and adaptation techniques, DEFIS+ will also directly contribute to greater awareness of climate 
threats and risk reduction processes. Furthermore, collaboration with MFIs may develop complem entarities between 
financial and non -financial services to agriculture, by defining the respect of technical aspects as a condition to 
access MFI funding. By promoting integrated financial services, MFIs will contribute to a positive potential impact on 
agriculture productivity and the sustainability of climate change adaptation gains.  
The adoption of the Green Roads for Water approach through capacity building of the technicians in various 
Government institutions will provide a pathway for scaling up the results of climate proofed road infrastructure into 
additional regions in Madagascar. The GR 4W approach for DEFIS+ will also contribute to enhancing aquifer 
productivity , which will have positive impacts on agriculture in the part of the country that faces climate change 
impacts on water availability and socio -economic activities.  
Through these interventions, DEFIS+ will directly improve the climate resilience of 447,201 individual beneficiaries 
and indirectly impact the lives of 3,543,719 beneficiaries in the six regions of the project. Improving their ability to 
sustainably combat the negative effects of climate change, produce greater yields and gain access to markets. This


---
*Page 56*
---

D 
  GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.0 | PAGE 52 
   
 results in greater income generation for rural communities, resulting in reduced dependence on the exploitation of 
natural resources in lean periods.  
DEFIS+ contributes effectively and efficiently to the Updated Strategic Plan (USP -2) for the GCF 2024 –2027 (Table 
7B). The USP -2 reflects a commitment to smart, transformative action in the face of the climate crisis. By addressing 
the complexities of climate change through enhanced adaptation, including mitigation, social equity, private sector 
engagement, and rigorous  evaluation, the GCF is poised to play a crucial role in catalysing global efforts to build a 
sustainable and resilient future.  The USP -2 builds upon the successes and lessons learned from its predecessor 
(USP -1), emphasizing a comprehensive and integrated approach to climate finance. At its core, the strategic plan 
sets some important key objectives aimed at accelerating climate action, fostering resilience and promoting 
sustainable development. In this context, DE FIS+ plays an important and crucial role in contributing to the USP -2 
strategic vision and collective determination in the pursuit of a climate -resilient world.

D.2. Paradigm shift potential (max. 500 words, approximately 1 page)  
Several projects in the south of Madagascar will benefit from the DEFIS+ project because this project will contribute 
to transforming the climate vulnerable, and unsustainable agricultural sector into a climate -resilient, market -oriented 
with modern infras tructure, and profitable economic sector, which also significantly contributes to mitigating climate 
shocks and improving food and nutrition, security, gender equality and empowerment. The structural and capacity 
transformation will be achieved by addressi ng vulnerabilities to climate change and supporting all stakeholders to 
improve their resilience, efficiency and links to markets. The paradigm shift for a green and sustainable climate 
resilience society that DEFIS + supports entails:  
● Climate proofing basic production and market related infrastructure to improve their resilience. The integrated 
watershed and catchment management approach will scale up national experiences in bottom -up participatory 
watershed and catchment management with the aim of both protecting natural resources and increasing income. 
Community - driven sustainable land and water management plans and activities will be supported. The planning 
process, which includes social engineering for behavioural change and impleme ntation of the plans will also enable 
knowledge sharing and joint learning among peers in the WUAs and the FFS.  
● The partnerships mobilised for DEFIS+, the scale of productive investments planned for sustainable 
agricultural development and the focus on building local capacities constitute a major paradigm shift in regions in 
southern Madagascar renowned for humanita rian and emergency assistance. The focus on scaling up well -tested 
approaches and technologies building on innovative approaches and lessons learned from previous IFAD 
investments (including DEFIS and other IFAD projects in the country) is an important fea ture of the proposed 
interventions.  
● Access to stress tolerant (drought, heat, flooding, salinity, etc.) and nutritionally enhanced varieties and 
planting materials developed by research centres. The production, multiplication and dissemination at community 
level of these climate resilient te chnologies will be promoted as will the adoption of integrated pest management and 
other agroecological approaches. Supporting national research centres and the FFS will ensure the enabling 
environment for improved seeds, promotion and adoption by smallholders.


---
*Page 58*
---

D 
  GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.0 | PAGE 54 
   
 ● Greening road infrastructure will have an overall positive impact on agriculture, water availability and soil 
erosion associated with running surface water. Thus, maintenance and restoration of green roads will have a minimal 
environmental impact and large  benefits for civil society.  
● Development of agrometeorological information. Due to the dynamic nature of exposure and vulnerability, 
impact scenarios for crops, land use and agri -infrastructure will vary widely. The project will use the DSS platform to 
understand risk (using hazard ex posure and vulnerability), including gender -related considerations and applications 
such as Met Apps and others provided by the Regional Meteorological Directorate.  
At the national level, DEFIS+ as well as other projects supported by IFAD, will contribute to the implementation of 
the strategy laid out in the National Policy for Action on Climate Change and the NDC (2015) that aim to support the 
development of sustainable, integrated and resilient agriculture models and water resources management.  More 
specifically, the adaptation practices promoted by DEFIS+ respond to the UNFCCC Technology Needs Assessment 
(TNA) developed for Madagascar.71 The TNA identifies the following initiatives as having potential for paradigm 
shifting: optimization of water management and drip irrigation; adoption of landscape conservation techniques; 
technical and financial support for risk management; adoption of ad apted farming techniques and stress -tolerant 
varieties; rehabilitation of irrigation infrastructure; and diffusion of drainage systems. Further assessments focus 
particularly on the promotion of smallholder agroforestry; and the development of resilient ri ce technology 
packages.72 DEFIS+ will also contribute to the regulatory framework for irrigation infrastructure through a focus on 
strengthening the capacities and standards of design offices and construction companies. The project is in line with 
the above -cited analyses, as it promotes the adoption of resilient agroforestry and landscape management systems, 
the adoption of  Systems of Rice Intensification (SRI), the adoption of drought - and stress -tolerant, and better adapted 
to climate change seeds and the reinforcement of irrigation networks as key strategies to improve the climate 
resilience of agricultural production systems.  
The project will shift agriculture in the country’s target areas from its current situation of high vulnerability toward an 
alternative paradigm wherein better information and climate smart  farming practices will significantly increase 
resilience to climate change. The reliance on state -of-the-art GIS tools will support strategic planning through the 
provision of more systematic data, which will act as a springboard for science -based decisio n-making processes. 
The project constitutes an important step in a longer -term process of transforming agriculture in Madagascar. The 
sheer magnitude of the required behavioural change, among a large and poor population highly dependent on 
agricultural out put, is an enormous task that the government cannot achieve on its own.  
This model can easily be applied to other geographic areas in Madagascar or other countries. The approach and the 
tools to be utilised constitute a "package" that could be easily replicated in other systems in the country. The 
successful implementation of this project will result in a new, replicable, long -term and sustainable funding model in 
other landscapes that could enhance climate resilience, improve conservation, reduce land degradation and boost 
economic development. Outside Madagascar, the proposed  model of a land use transition investment fund is well 
suited for the most vulnerable countries on the Africa continent, where unsustainable agriculture takes up an 
increasing share of available land, resulting in deforestation, land degradation and an agricultural sector not resilient 
to climate change. Thus, this model could be replicated (but not e xclusively) in other least developed countries in 
Africa that need large -scale climate investments in rural areas to finance the activities necessary to achie ve the 
objectives set in their NDCs and in their national adaptation programs.  
D.3. Sustainable development (max. 500 words, approximately 1 page)  
DEFIS+ will be implemented by the Government of Madagascar, particularly M INAE , the Ministry of Environment 
and MEF in close synergy with other rural and agricultural development investments intervening in the southern 
regions of Madagascar. Like other IFAD -funded projects, its interventions will re ly on using national systems at both 
the central and decentralised levels, and on strengthening the capacity of targeted poor smallholders and their local 
institutions such as Farmers’ Organisations, in order to better empower the rural poor, with a particular focus on 
women and youth. When looking at ensuring sustainable development, climate information represents an essential 
element for increasing the capacity of vulnerable households to adapt to climate variability.  
The DEFIS+ project includes important sustainable co -benefi ts:

 Environmental co -benefits. DEFIS+ will promote sustainable and climate -resilient agricultural practices, 
technologies and infrastructures that contribute to improving the efficient management and use of water resources 
in agriculture, enhancing soil quality and restoring degraded lands. Its integrated watershed management approach 
will help increase moisture content in the soil, optimize the use of water and expand the vegetative cover of degraded 
lands, while reducing thei r wind - and rainfall -induced erosion. It will also help reduce the siltation of water bodies, 
hydro -agricultural infrastructure and irrigation canals. This will, in turn, lead to greater biodiversity in the target areas 
and surrounding landscapes, thereby maintaining key eco systems services that are crucial for agricultural production, 
especially in the context of climate change.  
Practices that increase soil organic matter also help increase carbon sequestration, reduce GHG emissions and 
improve soil nutrient availability and water -holding capacity, which in turn strengthens the resilience of farming 
systems and increases productiv ity. Sustainable soil fertility management will allow the mitigation of climate change 
due to the improved  carbon sequestration capacity of soils rich in organic material. Agroforestry and silvopastoral 
systems can increase livestock productivity by reducing heat stress to animals. Thus, the application of good livestock 
and manure management practices results  in a reduction of methane emissions compared with the current BAU. In 
addition, trees increase carbon storage in soils and biomass. Watershed rehabilitation increases carbon stored in 
the land and the use of renewable energy technologies will reduce carbo n emissions. The process of manufacturing 
biogas through anaerobic digestion makes it possible to reduce the sources of methane production and to 
concentrate them in one place, in addition to the other advantages of valorisation  of agricultural by -products, but also 
to reduce the use of wood, which is also a source of household energy. Improved stoves will also reduce pressure 
on forest products.  
Social co -benefits including health impacts . Higher food security and nutrition will be among the key social co -
benefits that will arise. The proportion of targeted households with sufficient food intake is expected to increase from 
50 to 90%. In addition, 45,000 households will benefit from nutritional education, which will improve women’s and 
infants’ health. Better access to water, more effective watershed management and the use of off -farm technologies 
will reduce women’s time burden and workload. Women's  and youth’s employment opportunities  will increase, and 
women’s participation in the rural economy will be  further  formalised and recognized. By increasing and diversifying 
income sources, as well as improving food security, the project will also contribute to reducing migration flows to 
urban areas.  
Specific actions proposed for women and young people in vulnerable situations to facilitate their access to productive 
capital and to income -generating activities or jobs include: (i) inclusion of young people trained by FORMAPROD by 
reserving them plots w ithin the newly developed perimeters; (ii) positioning of young people and women as rural 
entrepreneurs involved in the service professions linked to development poles; (iii) prioritisation of young people and 
women in capacity building actions and access to financing for producers. Young people will be trained not only on 
production but also on processing, storage, marketing, sale/distribution of agricultural inputs, management of 
irrigated areas and advisory services  as well as maintenance of new technologies and equipment .  
Economic co -benefits . DEFIS+ will contribute to reduce poverty by supporting the diversification of agricultural 
production, building the climate resilience of production systems and infrastructures and creating market linkages, 
which will reduce transaction costs and boost th e local economy. The sustainable management of agricultural water 
within an integrated watershed approach, coupled with more climate and stress -tolerant  seeds, will sustainably 
increase agricultural productivity. In addition, the dissemination of climate resilient practices and technologies will 
enhance the adaptive capacity of agricultural systems and the income of smallholder  farmers  and their households . 
The activities planned, based on previous experiences, that contribute to the increase in productivity are efficient 
irrigation, optimal organic fertilizer use, improvement of agricultural practices through FFS, use of improved and 
certified seeds that are climate resilient.  
Owing to increased productivity and improved linkages to the  market, the income of the targeted smallholders will 
increase. At least 53,900 small -scale farmers are expected to adopt more resilient agricultural technologies. Based 
on conservative assumptions, the Economic I nternal Rate of Return (E-IRR) of DEFIS+ is predicted to be 24% and 
its Economic Net P resent Value (E-NPV)  is expected  to reach USD 255.5 million (see Annex 3, Economic and 
Financial Analysis).  
Mitigation co -benefits . The co -benefits of integrating a mitigation component into the DEFIS+ project are diverse 
and impactful. From environmental sustainability to operational efficiency, cost -effectiveness, stakeholder 
engagement, and social responsibility, climate change mitigation contributes to the holistic success and positive 
impact of this project. Co-benefits include the restoration of degraded and marginal areas and sustainable 
agroforestry practises that contribute to carbon sequestration and climate  change  mitigation. The Project also  
introduces innovative, low -cost, context -specific renewable energy and energy -efficient technologies for farming and 
processing (such as solar pumps and dryers) and domestic activities (such as improved cooking stoves to reduce 
the use of firewood), which  contribute to reducing GHG emissions. Thus, climate change mitigation co -benefit

 activities will contribute to NDC priorities, which include facilitating access to energy by strengthening existing 
systems and promoting renewable energy and green technolog ies. 
Gender -sensitive development impacts  DEFIS+ will implement a gender strategy to ensure women’s specific 
needs are considered  at all stages of implementation and will provide opportunities to improve their quality of life, 
reduce their vulnerability to climate change and increase their incomes. The activities proposed for women and youth 
will include training of farming households on gender issues  including the use of the Gender action Learning System 
(GALS)  and measures to increase their access to productive capital and income -generating activities, primarily 
access to land, with a view to positioning women as rural entrepreneurs and service providers in development hubs 
and facilitating  their access to capacity -building and financial services. The number of green jobs created with this 
project will target women, heading 30% of the selected households, in addition to w omen in male headed households, 
thus moving a step closer to narrowing the gender gap. The project will also involve minorities, vulnerable people 
and groups through the social inclusion strategy . 
The project is well placed to enable the achievement of the Sustainable Development Goals. The 2030 Agenda for 
Sustainable Development,  adopted by all United Nations Member States in 2015, provides a shared blueprint for 
peace and prosperity for people and the planet, connecting all countries in a global partnership. Countries have 
recognised that ending poverty and other deprivations must  go hand -in-hand with strategies that improve health and 
education, reduce inequality and spur economic growth – all while tackling climate change and working to preserve 
oceans and forests. The DEFIS+ project contributes to SDG 13 (Climate Action), SDG 1  (No Poverty), SDG 2 (Zero 
Hunger), SDG 5 (Gender Equality), SDG 7 (Affordable and Clean Energy) and SDG 15 (Life on Land).  
Social equity.  The overall strategy of DEFIS+ is to ensure social equity and cohesion among all members of civil 
society. Thus, this project follows an inclusive targeting strategy to reach vulnerable groups and poor households 
headed by women and youth, so that these t argeted rural populations, characterised by their vulnerability to the 
effects of climate change, will have equal opportunities to access factors of production (land, water, inputs, financing, 
etc.) in order to ensure fair and equitable results and enable sustainable impacts on living conditions.  
For gender equity, DEFIS+ will implement a gender strategy based on the consideration of inequalities related to 
sex, age and status. Such a strategy is based on the national policy, GCF updated gender policy and action plan, 
IFAD's action plan in this area and on the experiences and best practices developed by previous projects in 
Madagascar. The objectives of this strategy are to: (i) remove the constraints limiting the inclusion, active participation 
and empower ment of women and young people (men and wom en) in the different link ages of the climate change 
agenda and the agricultural sectors in order to create opportunities to improve their living conditions and coping with  
the changing climate; (ii) strengthen their productive capacities in order to increase the productivity and profitability 
of their economic activities by facilitating women's and youth access to resources and production factors (land, water, 
inputs, financing/credit, v ocational training, climate information, technologies, advisory support , etc. ); and (iii) 
promote women's leadership and their participation in decision -making bodies at the household level, in P roducer 
Organisations (WUA s, Cooperative etc)  in platforms/frameworks for consultation of the sectors and in local 
communities in general.       
D.4. Needs of recipient (max. 500 words, approximately 1 page)  
With a GNI per capita of USD 480 in 2020, Madagascar belongs to the group of least developed countries (LDCs). 
After real GDP growth of 4.4% in 2019, the country went into a recession in 2020, with real GDP declining by  4%.73 
The combined impact of global trade disruptions and domestic containment measures is estimated to have resulted 
in a GDP contraction of 4.2% in 2020, similar to that observed during the devastating 2009 political crisis.74 Led by 
the industrial and service sectors, GDP growth increased between 2013 and 2017, from 2.3% to 4.2%, whereas 
between 2014 and 2017, the agricultural sector contracted an average of  0.8% per year. In a country where 
agriculture employs nearly 80% of the population, this indicates that the economic growth experienced in the past 
few years did not have a positive impact on the living conditions of the rural population. The limited perf ormance of 
the agricultural sector is the result of’ limited means of production, low productivity levels, insecurity in rural areas, 
overexploitation of natural resources, vulnerability to climate change (cyclones, drought and floods) and constraints 
on access to markets  particularly in the rural areas .  
Madagascar’s ranking on the Human Development Index is still very low 0.528 positioning it at 164th place (out of 
189). In 2018 it went from 0.512 to 0.519, it fell three positions on the global index during this period, from 158th to 
161st place (out of 188 countries). Social and geographic inequalities in the country are still very pronounced: more 
than 80% of the population lives below the poverty level, with USD 2 per day, and 80% of the population lives in rural 
zones, sometimes in very  remote areas with limited access to basic infrastructure. Food insecurity and malnutrition 
 
73 https://www.afdb.org/en/countries/southern -africa/madagascar/madagascar -economic -outlook  
74 https://www.worldbank.org/en/country/madagascar/publication/madagascar -economic -update -covid -19-increases -poverty -a-new-reform -
momentum -is-needed -to-build -back -stronger

 exist in the country at high prevalence, affecting predominantly women of reproductive age and children, and creating 
huge socio -economic losses.  
The country is among the poorest in the world and poverty increased significantly as a result of the 2009 -2013 crisis. 
The poverty rate (at $1.9/day) is estimated to rise to 77.4% in 2020, up from 74.3% in 2019, corresponding to an 
increase of 1.38 million  people in one year.75 The incidence of extreme poverty (less than USD 1.25 per day) rose to 
78% in 2012. Poverty is clearly more severe in rural areas, where it affects 87.6% of the population, in comparison 
to 55.1% in urban areas. Extreme poverty is more pronounced in the so uth. The activities of DEFIS+ aim to restore 
and/or rehabilitate the soil and improve land use through a participatory approach with the local community. This 
approach will ensure that any potential land conflict is identified and resolved before any activ ities commence. For 
the rehabilitation of roads, a resettlement policy for beneficiaries who may be voluntarily displaced will have to be 
implemented before the works are initiated. Adherence to IFAD’s Social , Environment and Climate Assessment 
Procedures (SECAP) also follows the principles of Free Prior and Informed Consent that ensures existing inequalities 
are not augmented. A Grievance Redress Mechanism is also articulated for the project to manage any potential 
conflicts. In addition, DEFIS+ fully take s into account the needs of the recipient, which are in line with the national 
priorities. Furthermore, continuous engagement with the NDA has resumed, and DEFIS+ documents have been 
shared with the NDA. Further information is available in Annex 2a.  
Several studies have confirmed Madagascar’s high vulnerability to climate change (see Section B.1 and Annex 2b). 
The growing frequency and intensity of droughts coupled with increasing rainfall variability pose significant challenges 
to the agricultural se ctor. This results in very high exposure and vulnerability of the rural sectors in southern regions 
to climate -driven changes in rainfall patterns, rising temperatures and consequent declines in agricultural water 
availability. Such changes will adversely impact crops that are important in economic and food security terms such 
as rice, maize, among others. Furthermore, aridification in the southernmost regions will lead to greater wind erosion, 
while more frequent flash floods will exacerbate problems such as soil erosion, siltation of irrigation infrastructure, 
and damage to rural roads. Rising temperatures will also contribute to the proliferation of pests and diseases and put 
even more pressure on scarce water resources for irrigation purposes.  
The precarious economic situation of Madagascar entails that the implementation of the project’s activities is 
conditioned by the availability of external financial support, hence this request for GCF resources. The GCF resources 
will supplement the limite d national resources, while also ensuring the debt sustainability for Madagascar as a country 
that is highly vulnerable to the effects of climate change. In its 12th replenishment cycle (2022 -2024) IFAD has 
increased its targets in relation to climate change, meaning that at least 40% of its investments have to be invested 
in climate change adaptation or mitigation measures . Recognising the high demand and need for climate finance 
and a better integration of climate change adaptation  into project design  and implementation , IFAD support s countries 
is accessing  Global Climate Funds. In this context, the additional funds provided by the GCF will enable DEFIS+ to 
contribute to meeting such a target with a robust climate rationale, a sound climate risk analysis and a well -structured 
strategy to deal with the negati ve impacts of climate change in six target regions in Madagascar.  
D.5. Country ownership (max. 500 words, approximately 1 page)  
DEFIS+ activities will support Madagascar’s national policy for climate change, environment and sustainable 
development and the national strategies, such as the sectoral program for agriculture, livestock and fisheries. DEFIS+ 
directly supports the National Policy for Action on Climate Change by str engthening climate change adaptation, while 
taking into account the country’s needs. It will also promote the integration of climate change into all levels of 
policymaking, as well as research, development and technology transfer in this field. Madagascar is also at the early 
stages of designing a GCF country program (GCF/UNEP Readiness grant). DEFIS+ investments are fully aligned 
with key priority sectors identified in Madagascar’s National Adaptation Plan of Action: infrastructure, water resource 
manageme nt, agriculture and livestock.  According to the National Adaptation Plan, DEFIS + activities are aligned 
and perfectly integrated with the following priorities: (a) Strategic priority for agriculture. Promote resilient agricultura l 
systems. This will primarily involve supporting rural populations (men and women, including people with disabilities) 
in setting up an agriculture that is resilient to climate change, also helping to strengthen the fight against food and 
nutritional insecurity, with a particular focus on the most vulnerable g roups and on agricultural activities differentiated 
between men and women. (b) Developing pilot initiatives for the application of Integrated Models of Resilient 
Agriculture . Madagascar has a wide range of agricultural systems and agroecological practices in place: sowing on 
permanent plant cover, intensive rice cultivation (SRI) and improved rice cultivation (SRA), random irrigation rice 
cultivation (RIA), integration of agri culture and livestock, agroforestry, combinations of agroecological techniques and 
systems, etc.

 Madagascar’s Nationally Determined Contributions aim to build national resilience to cyclones, floods and droughts 
and give priority to adaptation interventions by 2030, such as the widespread application of resilient agriculture 
models; sustainable and integrated water management, including the mobili sation of surface water resources for 
semi -arid zones and the upgrading of irrigation infrastructure to cope with the reduction in runoff during dry periods; 
reduce GHG emissions and increase carbon sequestration; improve rice farming systems (SRI/SREA) and  the 
implementation of ecosystem -based adaptation good practices. Blue or green infrastructure solutions that integrate 
and interact positively with the rural environment which they are part of must be considered: alternatives to charcoal, 
fossil fuel, wat er abstraction systems, etc. The design of these infrastructures must consider  durability and use of 
resilient materials. DEFIS+ will support measures listed in Madagascar’s Third National Communication, such as 
improved soil management, the development of production systems adapted to climate change and optimal use of 
water resourc es. DEFIS+ will also contribute to  the implementation of Madagascar’s Nationally Appropriate Mitigation 
Actions, focusing on the multiplication of fodder seed production and outreach in pastoral areas and intensification 
of improved seeds production. It will contribute to priority actions defined by Madagascar’s National Capacity Self -
Assessment, including the involvement of regional/local authorities in decision -making on climate change, an  
awareness program to address climate change, training on good practices and solutions identified in its Technology 
Need Assessment, such as strengthening integrated water resources management and the capacity of technical 
officers in charge of water infra structures.  
Since 1979, IFAD has provided USD 404.8 million in financing to a total of 16 projects in Madagascar. IFAD’s COSOP 
focuses on integrated management of natural resources and increasing vulnerable rural households’ resilience. The 
evaluation of the Madagascar country program for the period 2000 -2012 carried  out by the Independent Evaluation 
Office of IFAD indicates satisfactory performance in terms of relevance, effectiveness, efficiency, sustainability and 
impact on rural poverty. Households receiving IFAD investments have experienced increased agricultural  production 
and productivity, and a marked increase in their cash income. DEFIS+ will scale up successful results from IFAD 
projects (AD2M, FORMAPROD, AROPA, DEFIS, and PROSPERER). The Implementing Agency  will be MINAE, with 
whom IFAD has been working for 40 years and which houses a permanent unit that oversees project implementation 
(CAPFIDA). During the program formulation process, there was continuous engagement with the National 
Designated Authority (N DA), which has endorsed DEFIS+ investments. The MEDD, which hosts the NDA, has 
confirmed that it is on board to support DEFIS+ and optimise synergies with other initiatives that it oversees.  
DEFIS+ has been informed by rounds of discussions and consultations with regional and national stakeholders and 
related formulation missions, all led by the Madagascar government. The consultations included specific meetings 
with representatives from the M inistry of Women's Affaires and focus group discussions with women. In addition to 
MINAE and MEDD, several rounds of consultation were conducted with stakeholders such as MEF, FOFIFA, the 
National Chamber of Agriculture, Federation of the Chamber of Commer ce and Industry, Ministry of Water and 
Energy, the Agriculture Development Fund, General Directorate for Meteorology (DGM), regional authorities, private 
sector entities, family farming communities, civil society and NGOs, WFP, FAO, UNDP, GIZ, CI, OFID and  World 
Bank, and farmers as evidenced by the list of stakeholders meetings held in Annex 7. Furthermore, the engagement 
of Farmers' Organisations in the implementation of the existing project defines an inclusive stakeholder approach as 
FOs are representat ives of the target group.  
During the studies for the rehabilitation of irrigated areas and roads, community consultations must always be carried 
out following the current legislation in Madagascar. For any activity and according to local traditions, meetings with 
local authorities and representatives of local institutions/organizations (in the form of participatory rural appraisals) 
should always be led by service providers in a participatory and inclusive manner. The stakeholders’ consultations 
on the CSA practices are no exception  to this approach. Stakeholders are consulted on the current practices for 
adapting to climate change in their agricultural practices. The traditional knowledge is then built on with the scientific 
projections of climate change and potential solutions are then discussed with communities. Climate change 
adaptation measures can then be prioritised as a result of the discussions with communities. The discussions are 
facilitated by technicians such as extension officers or community leaders.  
These sub -regional consultations also facilitate the development of a strong sense of local ownership over the project 
outcomes. They also occur throughout implementation as part of the M&E strategy in order to evaluate the program's 
progress toward reaching the expected results.  It is important to mention that an MRV analysis was conducted for 
both MINAE and MEDD and it has shown that the Ministries have the necessary skills and capacities to fully ensure 
the lead in the technical implementation and monitoring of DEFIS+ activitie s, outputs and outcomes. Furthermore, 
both MINEA and MEDD have managed large grants from international donors, and in the case of MEDD, it has 
extensive experience successfully managing funds from the GCF. Finally, IFAD has been working in Madagascar 
with the GoM, MINAE, and MEDD for 20+ years, and the partnerships and high professional standards have been 
nurtured .

 D.6. Efficiency and effectiveness (max`. 500 words, approximately 1 page)  
The overall approach adopted by DEFIS+ is designed to increase efficiency and effectiveness by focusing on the set 
of structural investments associated with the introduction of improved technologies and climate resilient  practices as 
well as the  capacity building of local institutions that will leverage growth in targeted agricultural areas. The integrated 
approach and integration of science and engineering for the in -depth modelling and understanding of agricultural risk 
will inform the structur al investment through the studies that will be undertaken. Furthermore, to move from low -yield 
subsistence farming to market -oriented agriculture, the project's theory of change is based on improving the 
productivity and adaptive capacity of production sys tems to climate change and beneficiaries’ access to markets 
under favourable conditions. Lastly, as the theory of change requires significant investments in basic infrastructure, 
priority will be given to the development of irrigation infrastructure and re habilitation of roads to reduce the risks and 
costs of accessing the nearest markets.  
The main expected benefits for people in the DEFIS+ project area are: (i) create climate resilient rural communities; 
(ii) diversification of income sources of the target populations, especially young people and women and green jobs 
creation in the project  area; (iii) reduced transportation costs through rural road rehabilitation, water storage 
infrastructure and market access; (iv) increased revenues for the rural poor people; (v) improved food security, diet 
quality, diversification and nutrition; and, above all, (vi) greater awareness of and protection from the climate change 
impacts negatively affecting the livelihoods of the rural population.  
DEFIS+ will offer several interrelated direct socio -economic benefits to 447,20 0 households in its area of intervention 
who have access to improved climate smart  technologies , farming practices and infrastructures.  The total farm areas 
to be cultivated with climate efficient technologies and practices is estimated at 16,000 hectares and 8,100 hectares 
of land in sub-watersheds will be treated to reduce their vulnerability to soil -erosion processes. The promotion of 
resilient agroforestry systems and landscape management in rain -fed upland farming areas will concern 10,000 
hectares. At least 150 water tanks/reserv oirs will be installed  and 540 km of feeder roads will be rehabilitated while 
strengthening their climate resilience.  
The procurement of technologies will be done competitively as outlined in Annex 10 (Procurement Plan) led by the 
PMU and MINAE and overseen by IFAD through the no objections that will be provided to the bidding documents 
and draft contracts. The goods to b e procured through national competitive bidding will be divided into lots and 
published in various media to encourage local suppliers including at the regional level to participate. The technologies 
will be distributed to the most vulnerable households in order to catalyse demand from other community members. 
Uptake by other farmers will be encouraged through FFS and demonstrations in the fields of the most vulnerable 
community members. Local entrepreneurs and enterprises will be encouraged to supply the te chnologies and 
strengthen the supply chain where this is weak. The cost structure will be grant based to the most vulnerable 
households while for the rest of the community members they will purchase the technologies. For technologies such 
as the solar ener gy, youth groups and associations will be engaged and trained in the operation and maintenance of 
the equipment as an income generating activity and support to their communities. Women and youth groups will also 
be trained in the manufacture of fuel effici ent cookstoves using local materials. The groups can then sell the cook 
stoves among community members for income generation.  
With the promotion of efficient irrigation systems, watershed management, and improved farming practices and 
varieties and planting materials that are stress tolerant  (rice, maize, cassava, sorghum, groundnut, small ruminants, 
pulses, etc.), DEFIS+ contributes to the implementation of Madagascar's international climate commitments. It also 
aims to empower and strengthen the institutional capacities of smallholder farme rs and their organisations who will 
be better equipped to undertake collective actions, such as managing and maintaining common infrastructures, or 
provide services to their members, and will benefit from the reduction in transaction and transportation cos ts derived 
from the rehabilitation of rural roads opening up remote villages. DEFIS+ with its innovative climate -oriented 
approach will benefit directly and indirectly 3,990,920  beneficiaries  (1,995,460 females and 1,995,460  males).  
The Economic Internal Rate of Return (E -IRR) of DEFIS+ is estimated at 24% with a Net Present Value (E -NPV) of 
USD 255.5 million (Table 8). These results indicate that based on an opportunity cost of capital of 8.5%, DEFIS+ will 
generate a satisfactory E -IRR and is more than justified on economic grounds . Further, details of relevant indicators 
are shown in Section E.  
Table 8: Economic Internal Rate of Return (E -IRR) and Net Present Value (NPV)

E. LOGICAL FRAMEWORK  
This section refers to the project/programme’s logical framework in accordance with the GCF’s Integrated Results Management Framework  to which the project/programme contributes as a 
whole, including in respect of any co -financing.   
E.1. Project/Programme Focus  
Please indicate whether this proposal is for a mitigation or adaptation project/programme. For cross -cutting proposals, select both.

☒ Increased resilience (adaptation)  
E.2. GCF Impact level: Paradigm shift potential (max 600 words, approximately 1 -2 pages)  
This section of the logical framework is meant to help a project/programme monitor and assess how it contributes to the parad igm shift described in section D.2 above by applying three 
assessment dimensions - scale, replicability, and sustainability.  
 
Accordingly, for each assessment dimension (see the definition per assessment in the accompanying guidance note), describe th e current state (baseline) and the potential scenario (target) and 
rate the current state (baseline) by using the three -point -scale  rating (low, medium, and high) provided in the guidance note. Also describe how the project/programme will contribute to that  shift/ 
transformation under respective assessment dimensions (scale, replicability and sustainability). In doing so, please refer  to section B.2(a) (theory of change).

Scale  Malagasy agriculture faces significant 
challenges, primarily marked by subsistence 
farming exhibiting low productivity per hectare. 
This is closely linked to the limited accessibility 
of services for producers, as well as poor soil 
fertility. The agricultu ral sector faces issues 
such as rural insecurity, the overexploitation of 
natural resources, and heightened 
susceptibility to natural disasters like cyclones, 
droughts, and floods. Furthermore, the 
remoteness of the project regions, coupled with 
deteriorat ing infrastructure, hampers Medium  DEFIS+ will scale up successful results from IFAD projects 
(AD2M, FORMAPROD, AROPA, DEFIS, and PROSPERER 
and transform the climate vulnerable, and unsustainable 
subsistence (mostly slash -and-burn) rainfed agricultural 
sector to a climate -resilient, market -oriented and profitable 
economic sector. The scale of IFAD productive investments 
for sustainable agricultural development and the focus on 
building local capacities constitute a major paradigm shift in 
southern Madagascar renowned for humanitarian and 
emergency assistance. Thus, the project will scale up well -
tested approaches and technologies building on innovative ● Rural development policies and strategies 
are formulated, implemented, and monitored at 
both national and regional levels.

system, resilient seed varieties and climate resilient 
infrastructures  
● Institutional and organisational capacities 
are strengthened in terms of sector coordination 
and monitoring of strategies for promoting priority 
climate and agricultural sectors and the delivery of 
services to farmers.

E.7. Monitoring, reporting and evaluation arrangements (max. 500 words, approximately 1 page)  
The monitoring, reporting and evaluation (M&E) system of DEFIS+ will focus on the results and outcomes identified 
in the logical framework and AWP/Bs. It follows the procedures clearly set out in the IFAD Monitoring Manual (third 
edition). This system is a lso used to assess the work of implementing partners in order to guarantee regular, 
systematic implementation of harmonised tools for data collection and management (databases, GIS) and reporting, 
as well as for knowledge management to evaluate knowledge c apital and identify good practices.  
 
A participatory M&E process will be used to ensure the establishment of a good understanding of stakeholders’ 
expectations and a common approach since the beginning. This will facilitate the appropriation and adoption of the 
information and results of the analysis generated by the system. This approach seeks to engage all stakeholders in: 
establishing a deeper, common comprehension of the objectives, strategies and appropriate information needs; 
organising collective moments of critical reflection to adjust  intervention strategies where needed; and using 
monitoring data as tools to support management and decision -making.  
 
DEFIS+ M&E unit is led by an M&E officer, with the support of a specialist in charge of monitoring climate change 
adaptation that will be recruited, a national assistant and M&E officers based in the inter -regional coordination units. 
The M&E officer on cl imate change adaptation and mitigation, to be hired with GCF funds, will be fully responsible for 
all M&E and knowledge management activities financed by the project.  
 
The M&E system will be used for: continuous, systematic internal monitoring of the implementation of planned 
activities and progress on different levels of outcomes, assessed on a biannual and annual basis; periodic internal 
evaluations specific thematic e valuations undertaken to respond to identified needs; and periodic external 
evaluations. The latter includes baseline surveys carried out at the beginning of the project and impact assessments 
at the project end to determine the benefits generated by the p roject activities.  
 
The quantification of adaptation beneficiaries (direct) is based on two conditions. The first condition is that the 
individuals receive direct interventions from the GCF funded project. The 447,200 beneficiaries will benefit from the 
following intervention s: i.) The development of efficient water management systems; ii.) Regular access to reliable -
climate data; iii.) The diffusion and adoption of resilient agricultural inputs, new technologies, and management 
practices; iv.) Improved climate resilience of b asic rural infrastructure; v.) Training and capacity building services; vi.) 
Knowledge management on the adaptation of food production systems to climate change and on carbon 
sequestration.  
 
The second condition is that these beneficiaries receive measurable adaptation benefits. All of these interventions 
aim to increase the beneficiaries’ adaptive capacity, which is a combination of assets and abilities that can be used 
to “prepare for and un dertake actions to reduce adverse impacts, moderate harm, or exploit beneficial opportunities” 
(IPCC, 2012). The above interventions will directly lead to measurable adaptation benefits, including increased 
resilience to climate change, increased food and water security, and improved livelihoods. The ToC describes the 
way in which the interventions will lead to these benefits. Food security, for example, is enhanced through improved 
agricultural production, while improved water security will be achieved thr ough better water management practices.  
The quantification of adaptation beneficiaries (indirect) will be a combination of primary and secondary data collection 
and extrapolation. Primary data collection will consist of surveys, and secondary data collection will consist of 
reviewing and triangu lating national census data and information gathered from government counterparts and other 
agencies in the project area. It is envisaged that the following beneficiaries will be considered indirect beneficiaries of 
adaptation interventions. Details are in cluded in Annex 11 (page 1 -3 and tables).  
 
Twice a year, the Government (MINAE, MEF, MEDD, and NDA) and IFAD will organise supervision missions with the 
goal of assessing progress on activities and the level of funds disbursement, the observance of administrative and 
financial procedures and the ab ility of the project activities to contribute to the achievement of the project’s objectives.  
DEFIS+ will publish on a biannual and annual basis a progress report that presents an overview of the activities 
carried out and their cost, the products and results obtained and the level of progress achieved on the objectives

F. RISK ASSESSMENT AND MANAGEMENT  
F.1.  Risk factors and mitigations measures (max. 3 pages)  
The DEFIS+ risk is assessed as part of project design for IFAD funded projects within the parameters of IFAD’s risk 
framework. It takes into consideration the country environment, PEFA reports, lessons learned from other projects 
implemented by the EE and diagnostics from other International Financial Institutions. Risk assessments are updated 
on a continuing basis throughout project life,  based on performance and audit findings.  
 
Introducing and adopting a paradigm shift towards sustainable and climate -resilient farming practices is a major 
challenge for both rural development actors and smallholders . The main risks that DEFIS+ will face during 
implementation are linked to: (i) the limited number and capacity of public and private service providers in remote rural 
areas; (ii) smallholder farmers’ lack of knowledge and resources to invest in more effic ient and more climate -resilient 
agricultural production systems; and (iii) producer s’ low level of organisation and integration into more lucrative value 
chains, which prevents them from obtaining higher prices for their agricultural products.  
 
Climate change risks, which have been increasing in recent years, raise the risk of poor harvests or crop failures and, 
as a result, of problems of food security and the erosion of rural households’ productive assets. The pro ject’s main risk 
factors and mitigation measures are presented in detail below. Overall, considering  the mitigation measures proposed 
below, the level of impact of the risks and the probability of their occurrence will be medium to low.  
 
Selected Risk Factor 1   
Category  Probability  Impact  
Technical and operational  Medium  Medium  
Description  defined in the AWP/Bs as well as the expected outputs and outcomes. These reports will be distributed to the line 
ministry, NSC members, the NDA and IFAD. DEFIS+ will promote knowledge and learning in all investment areas, 
including the design and developm ent of resilient agricultural infrastructures and production systems and efficient 
water management techniques. The overall approach to knowledge and learning relies on learning -based approaches 
such as farmer field schools, exchange visits, communities of  practice, learning routes, contributions to thematic 
networks, e -Library, training, and capacity support and on already proven knowledge management methods and 
tools. The goal is to generate a detailed repertoire of good practices in the area of climate c hange adaptation.  
 
DEFIS+ Outcome 3 is specifically designed to identify paths to knowledge development and build on partnerships 
between public and private entities to generate and disseminate further learning. The DEFIS+ M&E officer will be in 
charge of supporting these kn owledge management activities in close collaboration with the local IFAD M&E officer. 
Approaches to identifying and disseminating lessons learned will include the use of various tools, including websites, 
case studies, policy briefs, training sessions, kno wledge sharing events and workshops, radios, etc.  
 
DEFIS+ will create sustainable enabling conditions for both climate change adaptation and rural economy 
transformation by building the capacity of local actors and regional authorities to plan infrastructure (e.g., rural roads 
and water management infrastr uctures) and natural resource use in line with expected climate change impacts. 
Furthermore, through Outcome 3, DEFIS+ will support an enhanced policy dialogue and the development of evidence -
based policies. The GCF investments will lift the structural bar riers to resilient agriculture related to infrastructures, 
production systems and/or capacity, which will further contribute to create an enabling environment. Assistance to 
key rural stakeholders such as chambers of commerce, farmer associations and marke t organisations will also help 
lift institutional barriers and create an environment that is more conducive to a productive and climate resilient sector 
and economy. Annex 11 includes a full description of the Monitoring and Evaluation Plans.  Annex 11 incl udes a full 
description of the Monitoring and Evaluation Plans.


---
*Page 85*
---

F 
  GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.0 | PAGE 81 
   
 There are few public and private service providers in remote rural areas and the ones that do exist have limited 
capacity to intervene and few qualified staff members. These weaknesses may reduce the quality of support for 
producers and their appropriation  of the proposed climate change adaptation measures.  
Mitigation Measure(s)  
Carefully defined personnel requirements will be broadly publicized to ensure that the most qualified individuals are 
able to apply. Service providers will be selected based on their skills , expertise  and geographical presence . Partnership 
agreements and service contracts will be results -based and renewable based on performance. The staff of inter -
regional coordination units and the national office will support and monitor service providers regularly. DEFIS+ will also 
propose t raining for personnel responsible for giving support to rural producers.  The FFS approach, which promotes 
peer to peer learning and the capacity building of local technicians and extension services will also  ensure more local 
level expertise is available.  
 
Selected Risk Factor 2   
Category  Probability  Impact  
Technical and operational  Medium  Medium  
Description  
Smallholder farmers lack the training and resources needed to adequately manage the resources of their natural 
surroundings. Their level of organisation is insufficient to enable them to increase their productivity levels and seize 
market opportunities. The few farmers’ organisations that exist are often not very professional, act only lo cally and do 
not have the capacity to expand their range of activities, connect to services networks or integrate into more developed 
value chains.  
Mitigation Measure(s)  
Meetings and information sessions will be held with rural communities on the support that the program proposes to 
offer. The goal is to clearly explain the kinds of support offered, the expected results, the selection process and the 
eligibility conditions  for beneficiaries. The meetings will serve to clarify doubts, discuss the expectations and 
commitments of the different parties involved and build trust among group members and in the program. Mechanisms 
to strengthen communication and ensure transparent decision -making will follow IFAD guidelines. The program will 
support the organisation of the groups, training for their members and their access to resources to be used to develop 
services for their members. Small -scale seed producer organisations will be  called on to play an important role in the 
dissemination of climate -resilient varieties and species. The organisational and technical training sessions offered in 
the field and exchange visits shall help producers to improve their knowledge and capacity t o analyse the constraints 
they face and to manage farming activities.  
 
Selected Risk Factor 3   
Category  Probability  Impact  
Governance  Medium  Medium  
Description  
Tensions and conflicts over access to developed land, particularly the plots along the irrigated perimeters, may arise 
among people/farmers/herders and more vulnerable households.  
Mitigation Measure(s)  
Meetings and information sessions will be held in advance, before investments are made. These meetings shall serve 
to eliminate all ambiguities and discuss potential problems or conflicts that could hinder or even block progress on 
development activities o r the future use of developed plots of land. A preliminary study on the land tenure situation of 
the different plots will be carried out. Communities will be associated to the planning and management of the works 
based on participatory maps. In the plannin g phase of the works, coordination and management committees will be 
formed and officially registered as user associations. Traditional and administrative authorities and commune officials


---
*Page 86*
---

F 
  GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.0 | PAGE 82 
   
 will be fully involved in the planning and monitoring of the implementation of the investments since the beginning of the 
project.  
 
Selected Risk Factor 4  
Category  Probability  Impact  
Technical and operational  Medium  Medium  
Description  
Climate risks are severe, particularly in the DEFIS+ area of intervention, which is highly exposed to prolonged droughts 
and violent rains. The risks of sand accumulating in water retention or distribution works are high. Cyclones often have 
devastating impacts on infrastructures and crops.  
Mitigation Measure(s)  
The irrigated perimeters will be designed and built in accordance with the NIHYCRI and systems to protect areas from 
floods and erosion coming from the sub -watersheds will be strengthened. Infrastructures will be built in compliance 
with standards for prot ection against cyclones to limit damages. Training and support for WUAs will improve the 
management of water resources and irrigation networks and therefore, the performance of irrigated perimeters. Anti -
erosion works and improvements to vegetation cover w ill allow farmers to preserve and improve the productivity 
potential of their fields. Strengthening community management capacities is fundamental to ensure the adequate use 
and preservation of watershed resources.  
 
Selected Risk Factor 5  
Category  Probability  Impact  
Governance  Medium  Medium  
Description  
It is often very difficult for beneficiaries, the communes or state services to ensure the upkeep and good maintenance 
of rural infrastructures.  
Mitigation Measure(s)  
DEFIS+ will adopt an approach for the appropriation of the works by beneficiary WUAs that begins in the planning, 
monitoring and participation in the works and continues until the official handover of the works. At that point, a definitive  
transfer agreeme nt will be signed, which defines the responsibilities of the different stakeholders after the program has 
ended. In training activities for WUAs, the management and maintenance of the irrigation systems will be important 
issues to address. Finally, MINAE t echnical departments and commune officials will be closely linked to the 
rehabilitation  and monitoring of rural infrastructures.  
 
Selected Risk Factor 6  
Category  Probability  Impact  
Health  Low Low 
Description  
The WHO does not  consider COVID -19 a pandemic disease anymore, however, the risk of new strains exists and it is 
real. 
Mitigation Measure(s)  
In case a  new pandemic disease outbreak is declared by the WHO and the Government, DEFIS+ will put in place all 
necessary measures to mitigate the risk of spreading the disease.

G. GCF POLICIES AND STANDARDS  
G.1.  Environmental and social risk assessment (max. 750 words, approximately 1.5 pages)  
The Social, Environmental and Climate Assessment Procedures (SECAP) are IFAD’s main instrument for enhancing 
the sustainability of its projects and programs and include a risk assessment process that recognizes the heterogeneity 
of responses that exist giv en the wide range of circumstances in different countries and communities. SECAP are 
geared towards evaluating the risk and exposure of IFAD interventions to avoid, minimize and mitigate potential 
negative social and environmental impacts. SECAP also focuses  on climate change to assist project/program 
managers in assessing the potential damages that it will cause to IFAD investments and in connecting adaptation to 
risk management and poverty reduction.  
The SECAP describes  how to better mainstream environmental, social and climate change considerations into DEFIS+ 
project cycles and demonstrate IFAD’s commitment to go beyond the “do no harm” principle and to maximize 
development gains. SECAP seeks to ensure that IFAD’s poli cies, strategies and investments are designed to leave no 
one behind and achieve  sustainable development for  all, especially the poorest and most vulnerable to climate change. 
SECAP provides (i) an overview of the main environmental, natural resource management and social issues in the 
intervention areas, namely any significant impacts (positive and negative) an d social concerns likely to be associated 
with the project investments and services and (ii) the specific adaptation measures, envisaged on the basis of the risk 
analysis associated to the investments planned. The main risks identified (and corresponding m itigation measures) for 
DEFIS+ are: (i) adverse impacts of rehabilitation/construction work (hence, the application of national environmental 
and social impact assessment legislation); (ii) inefficient water use in rehabilitated areas (hence, capacity -building, 
coordination activities for WUAs, di ssemination  of water efficient agronomic practices such as SRI and its variants); (iii) 
erosion (hence, dissemination of anti -erosion techniques such as planting rows of shrubs and trees, strip cropping, 
shelterbelts; integrating cover crops, shrubs, soil improvers o r fodder crops into crop rotations); and (iv) limited access 
to project benefits for the most vulnerable farmers (hence, preliminary land tenure survey and proactive support to 
facilitate farmers access to DEFIS+ benefits). All activities identified in the  Environment and Social Management Plan 
(Annex 6)  are included in DEFIS+, according to the nature of the  activities and their  respective budgets . 
DEFIS+ project is categorized by IFAD as category B, as it might result in adverse environmental and/or social impacts 
on human populations or environmentally significant areas, although the impacts are less adverse than those for 
category A, site -specific  and can be readily remedied by appropriate preventive actions and/or mitigation measures. 
Category B projects require an Environmental and Social Management Plan (ESMP)  to be developed, which is the 
case for DEFIS+ . The SECAP also foresees the application of a national Environmental and Social Impact Assessment 
legislation (MECIE decrees 99 -954 and 04 -167) for infrastructures -related works. IFAD’s categorization of the project 
is in line with GCF environmental and s ocial risk category B, which includes activities with potential limited adverse 
environmental and/or social risks or impacts that are few, generally site -specific, largely reversible and readily 
addressed through mitigation measures.  
In addition to SECAP procedures, DEFIS+ is fully compl iant with the national regulations on environmental and social 
safeguards of the National Office of Environment (ONE). ONE is the Authority mandated to screen all investments and 
irrigation works, including rural road rehabilitation, in order to identify the specific regulatory requirements for each one 
of them, depending on their environmental, social and climate impacts. The Environmental and Social Impact 
Assessment will be validated by ONE, while the ESMP, will be validated by the MINAE. Over the years, the MINAE 
and its dedi cated Environment, Climate and Emergency Response Unit (SECRU) have developed extensive expertise 
on national and international environmental and social norms, particularly with regard to: assessing environmental and 
social strategies; monitoring environme ntal and social management frameworks; carrying out environmental impact 
studies; and supporting environmental engagement programs.  
The DEFIS+ Environmental and safeguards  officer will be the person in charge of ESMP implementation and 
monitoring. As already mentioned above, the PCU will ensure ESMP compliance with IFAD’s SECAP and GCF’s 
Environmental and Social Policy  during project implementation.  The climate change operations manager, and the 
Climate Change and Environment Officer in each inter regional coordination committee, will also be responsible for 
ESMPs implementation and monitoring throughout the project’s duration. For the protection of t he banks at the level of 
the hydro -agricultural infrastructures, vetiver, phragmites and herbaceous varieties will be used. Madagascar has 
developed Directives for reforestation, therefore the species and varieties adopted will depend on the pedoclimatic 
conditions of the areas and perennial trees as well as fruit trees, which are accepted by the beneficiary populations. 
The infrastructure activities will be screened for environmental and social risks based on the GCF Environment and


---

G 
  GREEN CLIMATE FUND FUNDING PROPOSAL V.3.0 | PAGE 84 
   
 Social Policy, SECAP and ONE regulations. Thereafter, site specific ESMPs will guide the risk management by the 
contractors and beneficiaries.  
A description of the program -level grievance redress mechanism is provided in Annex 7, together with a summary of 
the multi -stakeholder consultations carried out to present and gather feedback on the DEFIS+’s ESMP. The stakeholder

project implementation. Finally, Annex 7 presents disclosure -related information in line with both IFAD and GCF policies 
on this matter.  
G.2.  Gender assessment and action plan (max. 500 words, approximately 1 page)  
The targeting and social inclusion strategy  is based on the selection of intervention regions with high poverty levels 
and where soil degradation and the impacts of climate change hit the population’s productive capital the most. 
Furthermore, DEFIS+ intervention strategy excludes commercial farms a nd only targets individuals, especially those 
with a food deficit (type 3).  The targeting strategy was further informed by numerous visits and interviews with the 
communities and actors in rural areas.  
Gender considerations will be mainstreamed throughout DEFIS+’ activities. As detailed in the Gender Assessment and 
Action Plan (Annex 8), operational measures have been identified to guarantee that, while executing all programme 
activities, special care is system atically taken to ensure that women and youth from the most vulnerable beneficiaries 
effectively engage. Direct targeting will be carried out in beneficiary communities that have been identified to ensure 
that type 3 food deficit  households , women and youth are taken into account and have the opportunity to access the 
resources and initiatives mobilised by DEFIS+. Eligibility criteria – based, for example, on age, gender, vulnerability 
level, education level, and socioeconomic status – will be defined with the beneficiary communities through a 
participatory and consensus -based process. DEFIS+’ gender strategy,  is based on the need to address inequalities 
related to sex, age and status in the implementation of the project activities. Its objective is to: (i) eliminate the 
constraints that limit the inclusion, active participation and empowerment of women and you th (male  and female ) in the 
different links of agricultural chains; (ii) create opportunities to improve their living conditions; (iii) strengthen their 
productive capacities and the resilience of their economic activities to climate change; and (iv) promote the leadership 
of women and their participation in decision -making groups  and processes . 
The specific DEFIS+ actions proposed for women and youth in vulnerable situations have the goal of facilitating their 
access to productive capital through employment or their involvement in income -generating activities. These actions 
include: (i) integrati ng women and youth into agriculture by reserving plots of land for them in the newly developed  
perimeters; (ii) positioning youth and women as rural entrepreneurs in the services field in areas linked to the poles of 
development; (iii) giving women and youth priority in capacity -building activities and access to funding; (iv) promoting  
pro-youth and pro -women participation in green jobs.  
As in the case of the targeting strategy, the responsibility for executing the gender strategy  lies with the entire project 
coordination team, as well as the different regional partners and institutions in charge of project implementation. These 
actors will receive training on gender issues and on DEFIS+ strategies and tools for implementing, monito ring and 
evaluating the gender approach and ensuring the achievement of its objectives. With the support of the technical project 
manager, the entire team in charge of implementing DEFIS+ will ensure the integration of a gender dimension in the 
implementat ion of the activities. DEFIS+ will support the operationalization of gender strategy in relation to specific 
climate resilient activities with the aim of strengthening  the empowerment of women and young people.  
The monitoring and evaluation system will integrate the gender strategy into its operationalization. In the measure of 
products, effects and impacts, the monitoring and evaluation indicators will be disaggregated according to gender and 
age (men, women and young people). DEFIS+ will conduct specific studies on the impact of the pr oject on gender 
throughout its implementation (at the start, mid -term and end). The report will include the results of specific activities 
carried out by DEFIS+ as well as the results of actions carried out in synergy with IFAD projects in the country in r elation 
to gender. Information on the results of specific activities carried out by DEFIS + will be provided by its monitoring and 
evaluation system.  
The PCU will monitor gender mainstreaming, in particular focusing on: (i) how gender issues are taken into account in 
the project; (ii) the appropriateness of the gender strategy in consideration of the context and sociocultural reality of 
the intervention  zones; (iii) the disaggregation of M&E indicators by sex and age. This thorough exercise will enhance 
the narrowing of the gender gap in the target regions, paving the way for a scaling up of the approach at the national 
level.

 G.3.  Financial management and procurement (max. 500 words, approximately 1 page)  
MEF will represent the Government of Madagascar in accordance with Decree 2019 -093 establishing the Ministry 
attributions and its general organization. MEF supervises the planning, coordination and monitoring -evaluation of the 
country's medium - and long -term develo pment programmes at all levels and the coordination of cooperation at the 
national level. The Ministry also manages, monitors and assesses external funds and contributes to the harmonisation 
of cooperation with donors.  
MINAE will be the Implementing Agency  for the project and will provide technical oversight for DEFIS+. The NSC will  
provide overall guidance and validate AWP/Bs and evaluate progress made on activities and project results and their 
alignment with rural sector strategies. Regional guidance  will be provided by the RSMCs , who will also monitor the 
regional progress of implementation . The relevant departments of the RSMCs are represented in the NSC and have 
real decision -making power with regard to the mobilisation of resources, compliance with planning and implementation 
of action plans.  
Within MINAE, the PCU’s main function is to execute the overall programming and budgeting and to take the lead in 
overall project implementation, which will include working with service providers, government ministries and 
departments, individual project particip ants and farmers’ organisations in the target areas. Specifically, the PCU will 
assume responsibility for the timely preparation of the AWP/Bs and Procurement Plans to be submitted for review and 
approval (and subsequently to IFAD for its “no -objection”). The PCU will lead in the implementation of the project’s 
three components and will work closely with MINAE, MEDD and other implementing partners, to ensure that project 
outcome s are  achieve d.  
IFAD’s operational model ensures continuous implementation support and supervision of project activities by providing 
MINAE and, more specifically, the PCU with the required technical and expert guidance for effective implementation of 
project activities and adaptative management , should any issues arise throughout the project’s lifecycle. IFAD’’s No 
Objection will be required for the approval of AWP/Bs, which will be screened to ensure project activities build towards 
successful project completion in an effective and efficient ma nner. In compliance with IFADs Handbook on Financial 
Reporting  and Auditing, adequate financial management arrangements (staff, budget, funds flow, internal control, 
audits) will be secured for efficiently managing GCF funds, including regular Interim Financial Reporting and liquidity 
forecast. At least  yearly, external audits of the  project’s account will be conducted, as will supervision missions for 
monitoring and evaluation. This will be done in collaboration with staff from MEDD/NDA. Finally, IFAD with support 
from MINAE and the PCU will lead a mid -term review to ensure alignment with defined targets or propose corrective 
measures, and elaborate completion reports and impact assessments. Specific requirements for implementation 
support and supervision missions are fully developed in the Program Implementation Manual (PIM), which will be 
amended to integrate GCF implementatio n requirements.  
As per clause 14 of the Accreditation Master Agreement between IFAD and GCF, the procurement of goods and 
services for funded activities, whether by the Accredited Entity itself, the Executing Entity or a third party, shall be done 
in accordance with the rules, policies and procedures of the Accredited Entity to the extent and scope of its 
accreditation.  
In accordance with IFAD Procurement Guidelines, each procurement plan will include IFAD's proposed contracts, 
procurement methods and related review procedures. During the design phase, IFAD undertook an assessment of the 
national procurement regulations t o ensure their compatibility with IFAD Guidelines. It was agreed that national 
regulations can be adopted for this program as defined in Law 2016 -055 on the Public Procurement Code of 25 January 
2017, except for the international competitive procurement, w hich follows the rules defined in the IFAD Procurement 
Guidelines. The Procurement Plan (Annex 10) includes the details of the procurement to be undertaken under DEFIS+.  
Specific applicable points will be clearly identified in the MPCs and the Procedural Manual. In accordance with 
paragraph 3 of Module F5 of the Loan Disbursement Handbook, International Competitive Bidding (ICB) will be the 
mandatory procurement method for  the following contracts:  
- Goods: contracts over USD 200,000;  
- Work: contracts over USD 1,000,000;  
- Consulting services: contracts of over USD 100,000 for firms and contracts of over USD 40,000 for individuals.  
In accordance with IFAD Procurement Guidelines, the Fund will carry out a prior review of the above if the contracts 
are equivalent to or above the specified amounts.
